Single source of truth for status board (§19), enterprise blockers (§20), backlog (§21), open decisions (§22), hard rules (§23), and references (§24). Section §24 (Premium Feel Campaign) and the broader PF-40 sub-campaign narrative removed — that campaign closed implicitly when local-first became the architectural baseline; ongoing PF-XX item tracking lives in `tasks/backlog.md` and `brain/active.md`. Section §25 (formerly references) merged into §24.*

#### §23.177 — Phone-only CSS discrepancy: grep the media queries for the selector FIRST (PM-953 — HARD RULE)

Six commits (PM-948→952) fought the onboarding header's mobile rendering by editing base rules while `@media (max-width:520px)` silently zeroed `.progress-wrap` vertical padding on phones only. Any styling bug that reproduces on phone but not desktop: `grep -n "@media" <file>` and read every block touching the selector BEFORE changing a base rule. Base-rule edits cannot win against an overriding media query.

#### §23.178 — Fresh vyve-capacitor clone: three gitignored artefacts must be restored before `bundleRelease` works (PM-954 — HARD RULE)

(1) **JAVA_HOME** must point at Android Studio's bundled JDK (`/Applications/Android Studio.app/Contents/jbr/Contents/Home`) — the system Java is too new for the gradle wrapper ("Unsupported class file major version"). (2) **android/local.properties** with `sdk.dir=$HOME/Library/Android/sdk`. (3) **android/keystore.properties + android/app/keystore/vyve-release-key.jks** (storeFile path is relative to the app/ module). Symptom map: missing local.properties → "SDK location not found"; missing keystore config → `:app:signReleaseBundle` NullPointerException (no message). iOS equivalent: Distribution signing needs a cert whose PRIVATE KEY is in this Mac's keychain ("Not in Keychain" certs are unusable — mint a new one via Xcode Manage Certificates); first codesign use prompts for the Mac login password → Always Allow. Apple PLA updates hard-block ALL uploads/cert issuance account-wide until the Account Holder accepts.


#### §23.179 — Any EF writing a NEW category of row into an established table: verify closed-vocabulary columns against pg_constraint BEFORE first deploy (PM-955 — HARD RULE)
Hit twice in one evening: members.attribution_source CHECK rejected 'coach' (23514 — only link|code|admin exist), then partner_memberships account_type+subscription_status CHECKs rejected 'coaching'/'trialing'. Established tables accrete CHECK/enum vocabularies that predate the new writer. Rule: before the FIRST deploy of any EF (or migration backfill) inserting a new row category into an existing table, `SELECT conname, pg_get_constraintdef(oid) FROM pg_constraint WHERE conrelid='<table>'::regclass AND contype='c'` and either conform to the vocabulary or extend the constraint in the same migration. A 23514 in live testing is this rule violated.

#### §23.180 — Sandbox egress 403s are NOT auth verification; supabase.co is not in the bash allowlist (PM-955 — HARD RULE)
The container's egress proxy returns 403 (body: "Host not in allowlist") for supabase.co — indistinguishable by status code from a platform JWT rejection. A PM-955-session smoke test read a proxy 403 as "verify_jwt working"; it proved nothing. Rule: curl from the sandbox can NEVER verify Supabase EF auth posture or reachability. Verify via Dean's browser/device, query_logs after a real invocation, or the deploy response's verify_jwt field. Check response BODIES before claiming any negative test passed.

#### §23.181 — Code patches never travel through bash heredocs; create_file then execute (PM-955 — HARD RULE)
A heredoc-quoted python patch script had its quoting silently mangled by shell interpolation mid-session (patch applied wrong content, caught only by anchor asserts). Rule: any multi-line patch/codegen script is written with create_file and then executed — never inlined in a bash heredoc. Anchor asserts (`assert s.count(anchor)==1`) stay mandatory in every patch script regardless.

#### §23.182 — Coach-scoped habit_library rows are active=false BY DESIGN (PM-956 — HARD RULE)
Coach-assigned habits are habit_library rows with active=false + created_by='coach:<partner_id>'. This is load-bearing, not an oversight: PostgREST embedded joins and the Dexie member-habits hydrate ignore the library active flag (so the habits render, log and autotick normally), while active=false keeps them out of the global Dexie catalogue hydrate (active=eq.true&select=*) and the settings custom-habit picker. Never 'fix' these rows to active=true — that leaks every coach's habits into every member's local catalogue. Any future full-library query must preserve this scoping.

#### §23.183 — \uXXXX escapes in ordinary Postgres string literals are stored LITERALLY (PM-958 — HARD RULE)
Postgres does not interpret \u escapes inside standard ''-quoted strings — ' \u00b7 ' ships as six literal characters, not a middle dot. When authoring SQL (especially plpgsql bodies) through tool calls, write real UTF-8 characters directly (· — etc.), or use E''/U&'' syntax deliberately. pm958b shipped literal escape sequences into member-facing notes strings; pm958c re-declared with real characters. Grep check after any function deploy containing non-ASCII: `position('\u' in prosrc)`.

#### §23.184 — Same-statement CTE writes are invisible to later CTEs — never build test fixtures in-statement (PM-958 — HARD RULE)
All CTEs in one statement share one snapshot: an INSERT in cte-1 is invisible to a function call in cte-2 AND to a DELETE in cte-3 of the same statement. The pm958 video-probe "insert → resolve → delete" one-liner both failed to resolve and silently left the probe row committed. Test fixtures that a function must READ need their own statement (insert call, then test call, then cleanup call) — or a pure-function refactor so no fixture is needed at all, which is the better answer.

#### §23.185 — Helper functions inside page IIFEs must be window-exported before any top-level code calls them (PM-964 — HARD RULE)
onboarding.html's submit path died for 16 days because PM-942 defined showLoadingVideo() inside the PM-820 boot IIFE while submitQuestionnaire() (top-level script block) called it bare — a ReferenceError that fired after the loading screen activated, outside the try, leaving a permanent spinner with zero network on every platform. When adding a cross-cutting helper during a refactor of a multi-script-block page: export it (`window.fn = fn`), wrap purely-cosmetic calls in try/catch at the call site, and persist anything retry-critical (like _lastSubmitData) BEFORE cosmetics run. Corollary: after any refactor that moves functions between script blocks, grep every bare call site against the defining scope.

#### §23.186 — Edge Function gateway traffic lives ONLY in function_edge_logs; edge_logs will show EMPTY for functions/v1 paths (PM-964 — HARD RULE)
Querying `source='edge_logs'` for functions/v1 URLs returns nothing even for invocations known to have 200'd (the pg_net-verified onboarding v123 calls are invisible there). edge_logs = rest/storage/auth/realtime; function_edge_logs = EF gateway, filter on `log_attributes['request.pathname']`, with method/status/UA/execution-time in log_attributes. An "absence of requests" conclusion drawn from the wrong source is a false negative that can misdirect a whole incident — PM-964's first pass nearly did.

#### §23.197 — Parallel sessions can SHARE one container filesystem; a sibling's placeholder sweep mutates YOUR working files (PM-989b — HARD RULE)
Two Wave-6 sessions ran in the same /home/claude. Session B's commit-time PM-XXX→PM-989 sed sweep rewrote Session A's in-progress files, and B created files A had planned, which read from A's side as inexplicable corruption. The tell: PM-numbered content you did not write, files you did not create, or byte counts moving between your own commands. Response: treat it as a sibling session, not corruption — STOP building, fetch fresh HEADs on all repos + the migrations list, diff any committed state against your locally-verified artefacts, and rebuild working bases from a pinned SHA rather than the mutated workdir. §23.26's pre-commit re-fetch is what prevents the brain-commit collision at the end — it fired here and caught B's sync landing minutes before A's would have.

#### §23.198 — Applied migration names are PERMANENT: never apply with a PM-XXX placeholder in the name (PM-989b — HARD RULE)
`pmxxx_wave6_calendar_content_drip` is now fixed in schema_migrations forever — the sed-sweep discipline cannot reach an applied migration. Either claim the PM number before calling apply_migration (fresh commits fetch across all repos, max+1), or accept the placeholder and record the name→PM mapping explicitly in the brain entry, as PM-989 does. Silent unmapped placeholders break the changelog→migration audit trail.

#### §23.199 — B2B arrears billing correctness lives in CRON ORDER, not code (PM-998 — HARD RULE)
b2b-billing-run (cron 68, 00:30 UTC on the 1st) bills the month just ended by counting members still subscription_status='b2b_active' at run time. The daily b2b-removal-sweep (cron 60, 01:30 UTC) flips period-end leavers OUT of b2b_active. The 1-hour gap on the 1st is the entire mechanism by which leavers are billed for their final active month before being swept. Rules: (a) never reschedule either job without re-deriving the arrears alignment; (b) any future rewrite of either function must preserve bill-before-sweep on the 1st; (c) the idempotency guard is `uq_cc_invoices_employer_period` — manual CC runs and the cron can never double-invoice, so re-running is always safe, but re-running AFTER the sweep on the 1st undercounts leavers (the window, once missed, is gone for that month).

#### §23.200 — EVERY Edge Function deploy is unverified until re-fetched or invoked (PM-999 — HARD RULE)
An MCP deploy_edge_function call replaces the ENTIRE function and returns 200 regardless of whether the payload was the intended source — PM-999 shipped a literal placeholder body to trial-end-email (caught and redeployed within the minute; zero traffic, next cron hours away). A wrong-content deploy is silent: the function stays ACTIVE and bricked until its next invocation, which for cron-only functions can be hours later and fails invisibly. Rules: (a) immediately after EVERY deploy, either re-fetch the source (platform get_edge_function) and confirm it is the intended code, or run a real invocation (dry_run where available) — before ANY other work; (b) never stage file content in one step intending to substitute it into the deploy call later — the deploy call carries the real source or it doesn't happen; (c) this extends the existing prod-EF verify discipline from “verify behaviour” to “verify the deploy itself”.



#### §23.194 — Fetch-then-rewrite pipelines: CONTENT-ASSERT the fetched body before using it (PM-976 — HARD RULE)
PM-954c's split pipeline fetched partner-portal.html pinned to a bad ref, got GitHub's 404 JSON back as a successful-looking response body, and committed the error JSON as the live file — the partner portal served raw JSON for ~35h until Cole hit it. §23.190's set -e cannot catch this: nothing failed, the wrong bytes just flowed through. Rules: (a) any file fetched for rewrite must be asserted for expected shape before patching (HTML → starts <!doctype/<html; JS → node --check; plus a floor size sanity vs the tree's known blob size); (b) fetch by ref must verify the ref exists first or use the fresh-HEAD SHA just fetched (§23.21) — never a SHA from memory/scrollback (7706957a matched nothing in any repo; it was a phantom); (c) the md5 post-commit verify (§23.30) verifies WHAT YOU MEANT TO WRITE was written — it is not a defence when the local source itself is corrupt, so the assert must run at fetch time, not commit time; (d) diagnostic tell: a "site page" rendering as JSON is the serving file's literal content, not a server error — check the blob before the infrastructure.

#### §23.205 — A min-width rung must be the LAST rule in its block, because inverting a max-width query promotes the mobile declarations into the base (PM-1017 — HARD RULE)

Converting `@media (max-width:X)` to `@media (min-width:X)` is not a swap of the query — it moves every declaration in that block into the base rules, where they now apply at ALL widths and must be released explicitly at the rung. Wave 4 got the release right on tasks.html and its POSITION wrong: `.tk-col`'s base rule sat ten lines below the `min-width:900` rung, so the promoted `min-width:80vw`/`max-width:80vw` beat the rung's `min-width:0`/`max-width:none` on source order at equal specificity, and the desktop kanban board rendered ~4100px wide with three of four columns off-screen. **Rules:** (a) the min-width rung goes at the END of the style block, after every base rule it is meant to override — mobile-first authoring is a source-order contract, not just a query direction; (b) enumerate every declaration the inversion promotes and neutralise each one at the rung, including properties that were inert in the mobile layout but become load-bearing at desktop (`min-width`/`max-width` are inert-looking on a flex strip and catastrophic on a grid item, unlike `flex` and `scroll-snap-align` which really are inert); (c) after the edit, assert programmatically that no rule targeting the same selectors appears after the rung. Generalises to any cascade with ordered overrides — CSS layers, config merges, later-wins env files.

#### §23.206 — An `!important` at a lower breakpoint can NEVER be released by a normal declaration at a higher one (PM-1017 — HARD RULE)

components.css v2 clamped `.grid-3`/`.grid-4` to one column with `!important` at base and two columns with `!important` at the 640 rung, then set three/four columns at the 900 rung WITHOUT `!important`. Importance outranks both specificity and source order, so the 900 rung was dead code from the moment Wave 0 shipped it, and every `.grid-3`/`.grid-4` in the CC — 14 surfaces including the KPI strips on tasks, documents, active-users, complaints, bookings, dashboard, clients, competitors, compliance, intel, sessions, team and admin-console — rendered permanently 2-up at desktop for a month. Wave 3 inspected this exact ladder, described it as "`!important` at base and 640, dropping it only at 900", and read the drop as deliberate layering; Wave 4 carried that reading forward. **Rules:** (a) if you clamp a property with `!important` at any rung, EVERY higher rung that needs to change it must carry `!important` too — importance is a property of the ladder, not of one rule; (b) never describe an `!important` that is dropped at a higher breakpoint as intentional without rendering it, because the drop is the bug; (c) when a responsive rung "does nothing", check importance before specificity and specificity before source order. Same trap in any priority system where a high-priority override at one level is expected to yield to a normal setting at another.

#### §23.207 — A change to a file linked with a `?v=` cache-bust is invisible until you bump the query in the SAME commit (PM-1017b — HARD RULE)

index.html has linked tokens.css, shell.css and components.css as `?v=PM-1006` since Wave 0. PM-1017 fixed components.css but left the query alone, so every browser that had loaded the CC since Wave 0 kept serving the cached Wave 0 copy on a normal reload — the fix was visible only to whoever hard-refreshed, which meant Dean and nobody else. Cloudflare Pages deploying in under a minute is irrelevant when the URL the browser asks for has not changed. **Rules:** (a) any commit touching a versioned asset bumps its query string in the same commit, for every link to it; (b) "it's on Pages, it's instant" is true for the deploy and false for the client — separate the two when reporting a deploy; (c) the CC has NO service worker, so the vbb/sw ritual does not apply here, but the `?v=` convention is its equivalent and must be maintained with the same discipline. Diagnostic tell: a fix that works for you after a hard refresh and for nobody else.

#### §23.208 — "Move this block to its page" is a MERGE whenever the page already defines the same selectors (PM-1016 — HARD RULE)

Wave 0 parked a `.ap-*` block and a `#page-settings` block in components.css to be moved to their pages in Wave 4. Neither was a move. action-plans.html already carried its own `.ap-*` block defining nine of the same selectors, and because the router injects a page fragment with `slot.innerHTML`, the page's `<style>` always lands AFTER the shared stylesheet — so the page copies had been winning at equal specificity all along and appending components' copy would have created a duplicate conflicting set. Only four declarations components uniquely supplied actually needed to cross. The `#page-settings` block was worse: every declaration it carried was already supplied by components' own `.tab-bar`/`.tab-btn` primitives, so it was redundant rather than page-specific and was dropped outright — settings.html, a 7-script page with no style block, never needed opening. **Rules:** (a) before moving a block anywhere, diff its declaration set against what the destination and the shared files already supply, in both directions; (b) with innerHTML-injected fragments, page styles always win the source-order tie against linked stylesheets — work out what actually renders today before assuming the shared rule is in charge; (c) a block parked "for later" is a claim about the past, not a fact — re-derive it.

#### §23.209 — Off-nav is not the same as unreachable: check the router registry before migrating a surface (PM-1016 — HARD RULE)

PM-1012's lesson was to confirm a surface is wanted before migrating it. Wave 4 found the stronger version: seven of the pages its brief listed were not merely delinked, they were **unresolvable**. `router.js:resolvePage()` returns a route only for slugs present in `VYVE_NAV` or `VYVE_NAV_TOP`; `org`, `delivery`, `brief`, `knowledge`, `dashboard`, `commercial`, `marketing` and `action-plans` are in neither, so every path to them renders `renderNotFound()` — including four live keyboard shortcuts in shortcuts.js (`g d`, `g a`, `g s`, `g x`) that have been landing on Not Found unnoticed. **Rules:** (a) reachability is a property of the router's registry, not of the file existing or of a nav item being commented out — read the resolver before costing any page work; (b) dead routes still earn the minimum honest edit (retiring a shared name means fixing their refs) but never a full migration, and their breakpoints stay untouched under Law 1; (c) a soft-kill that removes nav entries without auditing shortcuts, quick-search and deep links leaves reachable-but-broken paths behind — grep those three when delinking.

#### §23.210 — A single md5 MISMATCH immediately after a commit is a stale read, not a failure (PM-1016)

§23.30 verification of a 10-file commit two seconds after the ref update reported nine matches and one mismatch; re-reading the same path at the same commit SHA moments later matched, and the blob SHA confirmed the commit had been correct all along. GitHub's raw contents endpoint can serve a cached copy of a path for a few seconds after a ref update. **Rules:** (a) retry a mismatching path two or three times with a short sleep before treating it as a failed commit, and confirm against the blob SHA from the tree, which is never stale; (b) do NOT relax the rule — every file still has to match at the commit SHA, never `ref=branch`, never first-N-chars; the retry buys accuracy, not leniency; (c) reacting to the first mismatch by re-committing or reverting would have been the expensive wrong move.

#### §23.211 — Fix a hardcoded literal in a shared file and you must assert it GONE repo-wide; the reported instance is rarely the only one (PM-1020 — HARD RULE)

Five design-system waves reported exactly one unthemed rule in components.css: `.error-banner`'s light-only `#843830`. A post-patch `assert '#843830' not in s` failed — the same literal was also on `.badge-red`, and pulling that thread found the entire `.badge-*` and `.pillar-tag.*` family hardcoding dark text over `--*-pale` backgrounds that are 12–18% alpha washes in dark theme. Nine rules, one of them (`badge-green`/`badge-amber`) rendering on `settings.html`, a live page. The waves each inspected this file and each saw only the instance they had come for. **Rules:** (a) when you fix a hardcoded value in shared CSS, assert the class of defect absent across the whole file, not just the reported selector — a targeted grep for `color:\s*#` costs nothing and is the actual audit; (b) a fault reported by a migration is a sample, never a census: migrations report what blocked them, not what is wrong; (c) **scope the residual assert to the REFERENCE form** (`var(--name)`, `color:#hex`), never the bare name — an assert on `'vyve-teal-dark' not in s` fires on your own explanatory comment documenting the retirement, which reads as a real failure and wastes a debug cycle. Generalises to any shared-file cleanup: deprecated API calls, magic numbers, retired env names.

#### §23.212 — When body-mounted nodes escape a page's scoped variables, widen the SCOPE, not the DOM (PM-1020 — HARD RULE)

§23.203(c) said to audit a scoped page's renderer for `document.body` mounts. This is what to do when you find them. partner-management appends three overlays to body, so the twelve page-scoped alias names resolved against nothing and the panels rendered transparent and borderless. The obvious fix — re-parent the nodes into the page — looked like two `appendChild` lines and was not: both modals close via `document.querySelectorAll('body>[style*=fixed]')`, a direct-child-of-body selector, so re-parenting silently breaks their own Cancel and Save buttons; and one overlay had been moved to body *deliberately* to escape a stacking context, so reverting it reinstates the original bug. Widening the defining selector to `#page-id, body` fixed all three with one edit and zero JS. **Rules:** (a) prefer widening the scope over moving the node — the node's own selectors, z-index position and event delegation are all load-bearing in ways the mount line does not advertise; (b) widening is only safe after proving the names do not collide: grep the shared files for both DEFINITIONS and REFERENCES of every name (§23.203(b) is the collision half, this is the leak half), and sequence any shared-file consumer's removal into an EARLIER commit so the alias cannot leak into the shell; (c) a page fragment's `<style>` dies with the fragment, so a `body` rule inside it is scoped in time even when it is not scoped in the tree. Same shape for portals in React, teleported components in Vue, and anything rendered outside its logical parent.

#### §23.213 — Before reporting a reachable-but-broken path, confirm the code that would produce it is actually LOADED (PM-1020 — HARD RULE)

I told Dean that quick-search could surface a record hit whose "Open in …" button lands on a dead route, having read `lib/entities.js` (six entity types with `route:` fields pointing at unresolvable slugs) and `lib/widgets.js:158` (renders that button from `def.route`). Both true, both irrelevant: `entities.js` is not in `index.html`'s script list — PM-759 soft-killed it out of the shell — so `VYVE_ENTITIES` is undefined, `recordIndex()` returns `[]`, and `recordModal()` returns null before rendering anything. The path does not exist. **Rules:** (a) a reachability claim needs three facts, not one — the code exists, the code is loaded, and something reaches it; grep the entry point's `<script src>` list before believing a library's contents; (b) §23.209 established that off-nav is not unreachable; the converse is equally true — **present-in-the-repo is not present-at-runtime**, and a soft-kill at the load layer is as total as deleting the file; (c) when a scan produces the finding, re-run the scan restricted to loaded code before writing it down. Own the correction in the same session rather than carrying a wrong claim into the brain. Generalises to dead exports, unregistered routes, uninstalled plugins and unmounted feature flags.

#### §23.216 — Claude CAN mint real member/staff JWTs for live proof: SQL-insert a temp `auth.users`+`auth.identities` pair, password-grant over pg_net, delete after; and pg_net cannot fetch Management-API `/body` (PM-1027)
`auth.users` carries NO triggers in this project (checked 2026-09-05), so inserting a throwaway user via SQL (`instance_id 00000000-…`, `aud/role authenticated`, `encrypted_password = extensions.crypt(pw, extensions.gen_salt('bf'))`, `email_confirmed_at now()`, plus an `auth.identities` row with `provider='email'`, `provider_id = user id`) creates a login-able account with zero side effects on `members`. `net.http_post('…/auth/v1/token?grant_type=password', {email,password}, headers {apikey: <legacy anon JWT>})` returns a real ES256 session token; `net._http_response.content::jsonb->>'access_token'` feeds subsequent pg_net invocations with `Authorization: Bearer`. The brain's earlier "Claude cannot mint member JWTs" lines (PM-9xx crisis-scan et al.) are superseded. **Rules:** (a) temp users get an `added_by='w0-test'`-style marker on any `admin_users` row so cleanup is a targeted delete; (b) delete `admin_users` → `auth.identities` → `auth.sessions` → `auth.refresh_tokens` → `auth.users`, then assert count 0; (c) keep the audit rows the proof wrote — they are the evidence; (d) never reset or borrow a real person's password for a proof — external partners' accounts are not test fixtures. **Second half:** pg_net stores response bodies as text and truncates at the first NUL byte, so Management API `/functions/<slug>/body` (binary eszip) comes back as 8 bytes (`ESZIP2.3`). For bulk EF-source greps use the Composio remote sandbox with MGMT_PAT (`strings -n 4` on each eszip); the `/functions` LIST endpoint is JSON and works fine from pg_net.

#### §23.217 — Column-limited staff reads are a definer `security_barrier` VIEW, never a column grant; and narrowing an RLS policy must land in the same commit as repointing every direct client reader (PM-1027 — HARD RULE)
`members` is read in full by members (own row), coaches (`is_coach_of`) and admin — column-level `GRANT SELECT (cols)` is per Postgres role, not per user, so it cannot give `team` fewer columns without taking them from members too. The pattern is `CREATE VIEW … WITH (security_barrier=true, security_invoker=false) AS SELECT <cols> FROM members WHERE is_admin_or_team()`, owned by postgres (bypasses RLS by ownership), granted to `authenticated` only (anon gets 42501). `auth.email()` still resolves inside the definer view because the JWT claims are a session setting. Then the table policy narrows to `is_admin()`. **The trap:** every client that read the table directly as the narrowed role now gets `[]` with HTTP 200 — RLS never errors (§23.166) — so grep `from('<table>')` / `rest/v1/<table>` across every repo BEFORE the migration and repoint those callers in the same change window (PM-1027 found two: active-users, broadcast). A view rename or column removal later is a breaking change for those pages; treat the view's column list as an API. Prove with a real JWT of the narrowed role against BOTH the table (expect `[]`) and the view (expect rows), plus anon against the view (expect 401) — a staff device is not a valid test (§23.214).

#### §23.215 — admin-* Edge Functions gate on `role IN ('admin','team')`, and any EF untouched since before a schema migration on the tables it writes is presumed BROKEN until re-read (PM-1026 audit)
`admin-member-edit`/`-habits`/`-programme`/`-weekly-goals`/`-programme-library`/`-dashboard` all shipped with `verifyAuth` = "any active `admin_users` row", restricting only `viewer`/`coach_exercise` which nobody holds — so the four `partner` rows (two of them external partners) pass. Any new staff EF copies the W0 gate, never the April one. Second half: `admin-member-programme` v17 (22 Apr) was "live" and cited as working while `workout_plan_cache` had since gained `is_active`/`surface`/multi-row-per-member — every one of its five actions was wrong against September's schema. Before citing an EF as "already works", check its `updated_at` against the migrations on the tables it touches; if any migration is newer, read the source against the live schema first. `admin_audit_log` with zero rows for an action is the tell that nobody has ever exercised it.

#### §23.214 — RLS policies OR, so an admin/team ALL policy silently widens EVERY member-facing read for staff accounts (PM-1023 — HARD RULE)

perks.html selected from member_perks with no visibility predicate at all, by design: `perks_member_read` gates on `active=true` plus the date window, so RLS was treated as the gate. But `member_perks` also carries `perks_admin_all [ALL] USING is_admin()` and `perks_team_all [ALL] USING is_admin_or_team()`, and **policies for the same command are OR'd — a permissive policy can only ever widen the result set, never restrict it.** Every staff account with an `admin_users` row therefore received the unfiltered catalogue in the MEMBER app, including hidden and out-of-window perks. Members were never affected. The damage was diagnostic, not confidentiality: Dean hid a perk, watched it keep rendering on his own phone through a full close and reopen, and reasonably suspected a cache. **Rules:** (a) a member-facing query must state its own visibility predicate; RLS is the security boundary, not the display filter, and the two must both be present — belt and braces, because they fail differently; (b) **a staff device is not a valid test of member visibility on any RLS-gated surface** — if a check needs to prove what a member sees, it needs a non-staff account or a direct query as that role; (c) when auditing a table's RLS, read EVERY policy for the command, not the one that matches your intent — the restrictive one you wrote is irrelevant if a permissive ALL sits beside it; (d) apply the client filter to every paint path, including cache reads, or the stale copy flashes back before the filtered fetch lands. Generalises to any OR-composed authorisation layer: Postgres RLS, cloud IAM allow policies, and permission unions in application code.

#### §23.203 — A page's local custom-property block is a WHOLE-FILE consumer question, and aliasing a name that collides with a token shadows it for the whole subtree (PM-1008 — HARD RULE)

Wave 2 was briefed as a CSS-only retired-name migration on partner-management.html. The `<style>` block was ~30% of the story: the mint-era names were referenced ~150 more times in DOM inline `style=` attributes and in the page's 124KB inline renderer, both frozen by Law 4 — deleting the theme blocks would have left the page resolving undefined properties across every dynamically rendered surface, invisibly, because an invalid `var()` substitution makes the whole declaration unset rather than erroring. **Rules:** (a) before deleting any custom-property definition, grep the ENTIRE file — CSS, inline style attributes, and every script block — not just the stylesheet; where the non-CSS consumers are frozen, keep a scoped, commented compat-alias block and retire it in the wave that owns those consumers (the §23.201 pattern, one level down). (b) **Never alias a page-local name onto a shared name it collides with.** This page's `--surface` meant tokens' `--surface-2`; defining `--surface` on the page root to bridge the renderer would have shadowed the real token for every descendant, including shared primitives rendered inside the page — components' `.tab-btn.active{background:var(--surface)}` would have painted the active viewbar pill the same colour as its own track and made it invisible. Custom properties inherit, so a page-scoped redefinition is a subtree-wide override of a global name, and it also poisons any sibling declaration in the same block that references it (`--card:var(--surface)` next to `--surface:var(--surface-2)` resolves --card to the shadowed value, not the token). Check what shared CSS consumes the name inside your subtree before shadowing; if it collides, take the value change and log it instead. (c) Page-scoped variables do not reach nodes the page's own JS `appendChild`s to `document.body` — those escape the scope entirely and have been resolving against nothing. When a page scopes its theme, audit its renderer for body-level mounts. Generalises past CSS: same trap for scoped config objects, shadowed shell env vars, and DB search_path overrides.

#### §23.204 — Inline styles beat stylesheets, and `#id *` beats every class rule (PM-1009/1010 — HARD RULE)

Two independent specificity faults sat undetected in partner-management.html for months and both surfaced as "the design system broke the page" when it had not. (a) **An inline `style=` attribute set by JS beats every stylesheet rule that is not `!important`.** PM-924 shipped `#page-partner-management .app{display:block}` to fix the port's layout; `showApp()` sets `#pt-app.style.display='flex'` at runtime, so that rule never applied once and nobody noticed because the symptom only becomes obvious at wide viewports. **Rule:** when a rule that obviously should win visibly does not, grep the page's JS for `.style.` on that element BEFORE touching the CSS — it is the cheapest check and it was the answer here. Where the inline value cannot be changed, override a *different* property that reaches the same layout (setting `flex-direction` rather than fighting `display`) instead of reaching for `!important`. (b) **A reset written as `#some-id *` has specificity 1-0-0 and outranks every class rule in your shared CSS.** Scoping a page reset to an ID feels safer than a bare `*` — it is the opposite for anything shared, because every primitive rendered inside that subtree silently loses whatever the reset zeroes, with no error and nothing in the cascade panel that looks wrong unless you read the specificity column. Write scoped resets as `:where(#page-id) *`: `:where()` contributes zero specificity, author styles beat UA defaults regardless of specificity, so the reset still kills browser defaults and now loses to class rules exactly as a reset should. **Diagnostic tell for both:** a page where shared components render structurally correct but visually stripped, or where one element hogs a flex line — check specificity and inline styles before rewriting anything. **Process rule the incident confirms:** when a deploy breaks a live staff surface, revert to the last known-good bytes FIRST and diagnose second; the page looking identical after the revert is itself the highest-value diagnostic you can get.

#### §23.202 — "components covers it" is a DECLARATION-level claim, never a selector-level one (PM-1007 — HARD RULE)

Wave 1's brief said to delete each page's local `.btn`/`.card`/`.page-*` "now that components.css covers them". Selector-for-selector that was true; declaration-for-declaration it was not. components.css defines `.btn:hover`, `.btn:active` and `.btn:focus-visible` but **no `.btn:disabled`** — blanket deletion would have left every disabled refresh button on platform/revenue/ai-usage rendering as enabled, with no error and nothing to see until someone clicked one mid-fetch. Three more of the same shape landed on the same wave: components' `.card` has no `margin-bottom` and its `.card + .card` never fires when the previous sibling is a grid wrapper; components' `.page-header` is a block with a margin that expects a `.hdr-row` child the frozen DOM doesn't have, so adopting it drops the header's flex row; components' `.stat-*` belong to the `.stat` card (34px value) and overflow a 6-up `.stat-cell` strip. **Rule:** before deleting a page-local rule in favour of a shared one, diff the two DECLARATION sets and the DOM contract each assumes — keep whatever the shared rule cannot supply for the frozen markup, tokenised, and delete the rest. A shared file owning a selector NAME says nothing about it owning that selector's states, layout responsibilities, or structural assumptions. Companion assert that catches this mechanically and did here: after the rewrite, every selector present in the old page CSS and absent from the new must be found in the shared files — any that isn't is a silent capability loss. Same trap for shared React components, base classes and DB views: name matching is not interface matching.

#### §23.201 — A "write this file verbatim" spec still owes you a consumer grep (PM-1006 — HARD RULE)

The PM-988 locked spec shipped a complete tokens.css v3 with the claim "names are backwards-compatible with all ~2,470 existing var() refs". Two were not: `--fs-xxs` (12 live refs across components.css, shell.css and seven pages the wave was forbidden to touch) and `--fs-hero` (2). Writing the file literally verbatim would have left eight pages resolving an undefined custom property — invalid font-size, silent inheritance, no error anywhere. **Rule:** before replacing any shared token/variable/constant file wholesale, diff the OLD defined-name set against the NEW one and grep the whole repo for every dropped name. Names with zero refs get dropped; names with live refs get kept as commented compat aliases retired in the wave that owns their last consumer. This is not a design decision and does not need an approval round-trip — the spec's own intent (nothing breaks) is the authority, and the deviation goes in the commit message. Companion assert for any CSS/class rewrite: extract the selector set before and after and prove it identical (frozen class names, PM-1006 ran this across the components+shell pair). Generalises beyond CSS: same trap for renamed env vars, shared TS constants, and DB column aliases.

#### §23.195 — NEVER embed JSON.stringify payloads inside inline event-handler attributes (PM-981 — HARD RULE)
log-food.html wired result rows as onclick='selectFood(${JSON.stringify(food)})'. Any value containing an apostrophe terminates the single-quoted attribute — in a UK food database that is half the shelf (Sainsbury's, Kellogg's, McVitie's) — so taps silently dead-ended, no console error, and it read as "search barely works". Double-quoting merely flips the failure to double-quote-bearing data, and HTML-escaping the JSON is fragile ordering-dependent hygiene. Rule: registry pattern instead — push the object into a module-scoped array and wire onclick="selectIdx(N)" (regFood/selectFoodIdx in log-food.html is the reference implementation). Sweep signal: `grep -n "JSON.stringify" *.html | grep -i "onclick\|onchange\|href"`.

#### §23.193 — Shared JS libraries must not assume the auth.js environment; onboarding.html is its own auth island (PM-972 — HARD RULE)
onboarding.html deliberately runs its own Supabase auth (session exposed as window._vyveJWT) and does NOT load auth.js — so any shared library whose getAuth/token path only checks vyveGetJWT/vyveSupabase silently gets null there, and downstream calls die at not_authenticated with console-only warnings. This made every onboarding-time HealthKit connect a native-permission-granted / server-never-told no-op for an entire night while four downstream bugs took the blame. Rules: (a) shared libraries acquiring a JWT must fall back to window._vyveJWT; (b) when a feature "works from page X but not page Y", diff the AUTH ENVIRONMENT of the two pages before anything else — it's the cheapest check and was the answer here; (c) fire-and-forget native flows (connect, baseline pull) must not swallow auth failures silently — at minimum stamp a diagnostic in localStorage; (d) new pages that self-auth must expose window._vyveJWT for library compatibility.

#### §23.192 — Server-authoritative hydration must never DOWNGRADE fresher local truth (PM-971 — HARD RULE)
PM-434 made connection state server-authoritative to fix stale-flag re-prompt bugs — correct — but its not-connected path cleared the local state cache unconditionally. When hydration is fed a CACHED payload predating a just-made local change (index's cache-first pathway), "server-authoritative" is actually "older-data-authoritative" and it clobbers reality. Rules: (a) local state changes with server confirmation behind them (a connect whose sync 200'd) get a timestamped stamp; (b) hydration downgrade paths must honour a grace window (15 min) around such stamps — keep the cache, assert the state, reconcile after; (c) the general principle: authority follows FRESHNESS of evidence, not source — a 60-second-old confirmed local write beats a payload of unknown age every time; (d) UI actions that depend on async boot (auth, hydration) must show busy state and surface failures — silent early-tap failure reads as a broken button and trains users to spam.

#### §23.191 — Local-first media on OTA bundles: NEVER trust the 'error' event for fallback — probe, assign, then play (PM-970 — HARD RULE)
The local-bundled-video optimisation (/videos/<key>.mp4 in native builds, remote fallback) hung its fallback on the <video> 'error' event. On OTA bundles the local file doesn't exist and the capacitor:// scheme miss does NOT reliably fire 'error' — the element sits on a dead src forever: no frames, no fallback, no network request (the diagnostic signature is ZERO storage GETs for files that should be streaming), and play() strands the controls in a playing-but-frozen state. Rules: (a) resolve the source FIRST via fetch HEAD probe of the local path, assign the winner, and only then start playback (onReady callback pattern — vyveVidSrc/setVidSrc); (b) keep the error→remote listener as belt only; (c) dev camouflage class #3: isNative false skips the local dance entirely, so this bug family is invisible in the dev shell and web — media-source behaviour must be verified on a store binary running an OTA bundle; (d) autoplay chains must never fire before the source is resolved.

#### §23.190 — Patch pipelines: set -e + assert-gate + empty-tree refusal before ANY commit (PM-969 — HARD RULE)
A failed python assert inside an unguarded bash heredoc let the pipeline fall through to blob/tree/commit with UNPATCHED files — producing vyve-site `d3e8435b`, an empty-diff commit whose message claims content it doesn't contain (md5 verification passed trivially because the files genuinely matched... the unchanged originals). Rules: (a) every patch+commit shell runs set -e; (b) computed tree SHA must differ from base tree or the commit is refused; (c) md5-after-commit proves upload fidelity, NOT that the intended edit happened — assert the edit's presence (grep the new marker) in the staged files before committing; (d) when a no-op commit does land, do not rewrite main — land the real content on top and flag the no-op in both commit messages and the changelog.

#### §23.189 — EF CORS must never pin to the dev-shell origin — dev testing is structurally blind to it (PM-968 — HARD RULE)
sync-health-data pinned Allow-Origin to https://online.vyvehealth.co.uk. Dean's server.url dev shell LOADS from that origin, so every dev test passed for four months while every bundled store binary (origin capacitor://localhost iOS / https://localhost Android) died at the same line: preflight 200 → WebKit rejects the mismatched origin → POST never sent → "Load failed" with zero gateway trace of the real request. Rules: (a) JWT-authenticated EFs use Allow-Origin * (no ambient credentials — nothing to protect with origin pinning); (b) any EF that DOES pin origins must include capacitor://localhost and https://localhost or it is broken in production by definition; (c) the diagnostic signature is preflight-200-no-POST in function_edge_logs; (d) "works in the dev shell" says NOTHING about CORS in store binaries — the dev shell is origin-camouflaged. Sweep of remaining first-party EF CORS blocks: backlog. PM-969 postscript: patients #2 and #3 were member-dashboard (allowlist without native origins + Allow-Credentials:true — with credentials, wildcard is forbidden, so the fix is ADDING capacitor://localhost, https://localhost, http://localhost to the reflect-allowlist) and get-health-data (pinned → *). member-dashboard's breakage was camouflaged by the REST/Dexie migration carrying page data — hydration-only consumers fail SILENTLY. When a native-only state bug appears, check EF CORS before client logic.

#### §23.188 — The first-run OTA fast path is a RACE, not a guarantee — baked binaries must never be trusted to self-heal (PM-966 — HARD RULE)
Dean lost the first-run race twice in one night on good wifi: install → move fast → the ~7MB sync hadn't landed → baked (broken) code all the way to the failure. The fast path (PM-706) is best-effort with a 60s cap and loses to a quick user or a slow link. Consequences: (a) any member-facing fix is only REAL for fresh installs once it's baked into the store binaries — OTA alone leaves a first-launch exposure window on every install forever; (b) when a fresh-install bug is fixed via OTA, the store-binary bake moves to the next natural Mac session, not "whenever"; (c) PM-967 resolved the design question with field data: the observed loss mode was FAST-FAIL (no overlay), so first-run now RETRIES 3× (0s/2s/5s) behind the overlay rather than blocking longer; (d) the trap in full: a first-run fix shipped by OTA arrives after the race is over — fresh installs run the BAKED live-update.js, so first-launch behaviour is binary-only territory, always.

#### §23.187 — "Verified by pg_net invocation" is EF-side proof only, NEVER client-path proof (PM-964 — HARD RULE)
pg_net exercises the Edge Function; it exercises zero client code — the 16-day-dead submit sat behind a green pg_net verification the whole time, and onboarding.html is additionally telemetry-blind (posthog init `false &&`-ed out, no client-error shim), so nothing could have surfaced it. Flagship member flows (signup, onboarding submit, checkout) require at least one real on-device (or real-browser) end-to-end pass after any change to the page OR its shared script blocks, and their pages must not ship telemetry-blind.
